)
.

E.5.1
Look Ahead HMC

The Look Ahead HMC sampler modifies the
Generalised HMC algorithm by
performing
extra
leapfrog steps when the proposal state is rejected.
This has the effect of increasing the acceptance rate for each proposal.

To see Look Ahead HMC as an instance of
Persistent iMCMC,
we consider the involution

{\Phi}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[0,1)\times\mathbb{2}
given by

\displaystyle{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{T})

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\displaystyle\frac{u}{\sigma_{j}},\mathsf{F})&\text{if }\max\{\sigma_{i}\mid i<j\}\leq u<\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\}\\
(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{T})&\text{if }\max\{\sigma_{j}\mid j\leq J\}\leq u\end{cases}

\displaystyle{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{F})

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}(\boldsymbol{L}^{-j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\displaystyle\frac{u}{\sigma_{j}^{\prime}},\mathsf{T})&\text{if }\max\{\sigma_{i}^{\prime}\mid i<j\}\leq u<\min\{1,\sigma_{j}^{\prime}\}\\
(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{F})&\text{if }\max\{\sigma_{j}^{\prime}\mid j\leq J\}\leq u\end{cases}

where

\sigma_{j}:=\displaystyle\frac{\zeta(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))}{\zeta(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})},\qquad\sigma_{j}^{\prime}:=\displaystyle\frac{\zeta(\boldsymbol{L}^{-j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))}{\zeta(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})},\qquad\boldsymbol{L}^{-j}:=({{\boldsymbol{L}}^{-1}})^{j}

and
\zeta
is the state density in HMC.

Note that in the involution,

u
determines how many sets (
j
) of leapfrog steps
are to be performed.
Say the direction is
\mathsf{T}
.
If the values of
\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\}

for
j=1,\dots,J
are marked
on the unit interval
[0,1]
,
then
the probability that

\boldsymbol{L}^{j}
is performed
can be represented by
the distance between
\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\}

and the highest of
\sigma_{i}
for
i<j
,
if it is non-negative.

Figure

10

gives an example of the result of

{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{T})

for varying
u\in[0,1]
.

0
\sigma_{1}
\sigma_{3}
\sigma_{2}
\sigma_{4}
1
(\boldsymbol{L}^{1}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\frac{u}{\sigma_{1}},\mathsf{F})
(\boldsymbol{L}^{2}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\frac{u}{\sigma_{2}},\mathsf{F})
(\boldsymbol{L}^{4}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\frac{u}{\sigma_{4}},\mathsf{F})
(\boldsymbol{L}^{0}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\frac{u}{\sigma_{0}},\mathsf{F})
(\boldsymbol{L}^{3}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\frac{u}{\sigma_{3}},\mathsf{F})

Figure 10:
Result of
{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{T})

for varying
u\in[0,1]
.

The Look Ahead HMC sampler
can be formulated as a
Persistent iMCMC sampler
with
the auxiliary kernel

{K}((\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}),\cdot):=\mathcal{U}[0,1)

and above involution
{\Phi}
.
Note that
the sampler always accept the proposal since
for
u\in[\max\{\sigma_{i}\mid i<j\},\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\})

with
j\in\{1,\dots,J\}
,
the acceptance ratio is

\displaystyle\min\{1,\frac{\zeta(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))}{\zeta(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})}\cdot|{\det{{\nabla{\Phi}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v},u,\mathsf{T})}}}|\}

\displaystyle=\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\cdot|{(\det{{\nabla\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})}})\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}}|\}=1

and for
u\in[\max\{\sigma_{j}\mid j\leq J\},1]
,
the acceptance ratio is also
1
.
A similar argument can be made when the direction
is
\mathsf{F}
.

E.5.2
NP Look Ahead HMC

Extra Leapfrog

Similar to the NP-HMC with Persistence,
we consider
the Persistent Multiple Step iMCMC algorithm
(
Section

D.3.7
)
that applies a random number of leapfrog function
(or its inverse)
to the current state
with the target density
w^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}):=w(\boldsymbol{x})
.

Given a
k_{0}
-dimensional
parameter

(\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
direction
d_{0}\in\mathbb{2}
,
a random variable
u\in[0,1)
and
a
dummy
auxiliary variable

\boldsymbol{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

are sampled from
the uniform distribution
\mathcal{U}(0,1)
and

K^{({n})}((\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}),\cdot):=\mathcal{N}_{n}
respectively
to form an initial state

((\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}),(u,\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))
.

If the direction
d_{0}
is
\mathsf{T}
and

u\in[\max\{\sigma_{i}\mid i<j\},\min\{1,\sigma_{j}\})

for some
j>0
where

\sigma_{j}:=\frac{\max\{w^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{t},\boldsymbol{u})\mid\boldsymbol{t}\in\mathsf{instance}(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))\}\cdot\varphi_{2n}(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))}{\max\{w^{\prime}(\boldsymbol{t},\boldsymbol{u})\mid\boldsymbol{t}\in\mathsf{instance}((\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v}))\}\cdot\varphi_{2n}(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})},

leapfrog steps

(\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}}\times(\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}})\times\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{n}})\circ(\boldsymbol{L}^{j}\times\mathsf{id}_{[0,1)\times\mathbb{R}^{n}})

are performed on

((\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}),(u,\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))
,
one update at a time,
extending the dimension as required
with a flipped direction
\mathsf{F}
.
Otherwise,

u\geq\max\{\sigma_{j}\mid j\leq J\}
and
no leapfrog steps is performed;

((\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}),(u,\boldsymbol{u}_{0}))
is returned
with the direction
\mathsf{T}
remains unchanged.
The treatment when
d_{0}
is
\mathsf{F}

is similar.

Say
the initial state with direction
d_{0}

is extended to a

n
-dimensional

((\boldsymbol{x}_{0}^{*},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{*}),(u,\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{*}))
and
is traversed to the
n
-dimensional new state

((\boldsymbol{x}^{*},\boldsymbol{v}^{*}),(u^{*},\boldsymbol{u}^{*}))
with
direction
d
.
The instance

(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})\in\text{Supp}(w^{\prime})
of
the
n
-dimensional parameter

(\boldsymbol{x}^{*},\boldsymbol{v}^{*})

is returned
alongside a flipped direction
\mathsf{not}\ d

with probability

\displaystyle\min\bigg\{1;\;

\displaystyle\frac{w^{\prime}{(\boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{v})}\cdot\varphi_{2n}(\boldsymbol{x}^{*},\boldsymbol{v}^{*})\cdot\mathsf{pdf}_{\mathcal{U}(0,1)}(u^{*})\cdot\varphi_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}^{*})}{w^{\prime}{(\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0})}\cdot\varphi_{2n}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0}^{*},\boldsymbol{v}_{0}^{*})\cdot\mathsf{pdf}_{\mathcal{U}(0,1)}(u)\cdot\varphi_{n}(\boldsymbol{u}_{0}^{*})}\cdot|{\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}}}|\cdot|{\det{{\nabla\boldsymbol{L}^{j}}(\boldsymbol{x}_{0},\boldsymbol{v}_{0})}}|\bigg\}=1

if
j>0
.
Otherwise (
j=0
),
the acceptance ratio is also
1
.

Note that the auxiliary variable
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}

has no effect
on the sampler.
Hence,

gives a SPCF implementation

ExtraLeapfrog
where
the sampling of the auxiliary variable
\boldsymbol{u}_{0}
is skipped.

NP Look Ahead HMC

Combining
ExtraLeapfrog
with
CorruptMom
,
the
NPLookAheadHMC
function
in

implements the
NP Look Ahead HMC sampler.
